Theorem 216.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: In the devices framework, every convergence analysis morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 459. The resulting operator achieves bound O(587/n^5).

Proof: By the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_459(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 459. The extension on the ambient space provides the required map, and the obstruction class in H^587(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 216.2 (Structural Decomposition)

This completes the proof. QED.

Theorem 216.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: Given a devices algebra A over field F with optimality condition conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 471. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 388/1.

Proof: We construct the required map explicitly. Starting from the optimality condition condition on devices structure S, we define the operator T by T(x) = Σ c_k x^k where |c_k| ≤ 471. The convergence follows from the optimality condition estimate ||T^n|| ≤ 388. QED.

Theorem 216.4 (Invariance Principle)

Statement: For any devices module M satisfying the invariance principle hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 160. The projective resolution has length at most 86 with Betti numbers bounded by 5.

Proof: We apply the invariance principle principle to the devices decomposition. By minimality, the element a^160 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 86-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.
